title: VQ 2.1 six-context distribution screen against corrected controls
tool: bounded VQ research diagnostics
---

The six new candidate forwards completed under the frozen resource and execution-source ledger, without retry or replacement. Candidate inputs are the same original-tokenizer contexts and last-sixteen-position requests as the prior corrected pilot. Controls reuse the hash-verified native baseline and corrected VQ 4.4 bytes. The larger VQ pack is a quantized proxy, not BF16 truth. Native parity comes from the separately captured full-stack experiments; the binary identity here identifies that checked implementation and was not the Python score producer.

Candidate mean case KL is 0.46529280439934456 versus 0.44401073962586735 for the installed baseline. Mean top-1 agreement is 0.6770833333333334 versus 0.7916666666666666. KL is lower in four contexts and higher in coding and tool-result contexts. Top-1 agreement is lower in five and tied in one. The continuation context is 0.5 versus 0.8125. These results do not support treating this smaller pack as similar quality for automatic promotion.

This is a screening result on six owned teacher-forced contexts, not held-out complete-task evidence, a confidence result or a universal ranking. No native throughput, MTP or vision qualification follows from it. All candidate processes remained below 10 GB and separately passed real-headroom preflight. The next engineering priority remains VQ 3.2; no product selection is changed.

Local home prefixes are replaced with <HOME>. Original byte lengths and hashes identify the unmodified local files. For large transcripts, the normalized UTF-8 bytes are stored losslessly as zlib-compressed base64 inside this Markdown source. Decode with `zlib.decompress(base64.b64decode(block))` and verify the listed normalized byte length and SHA-256. Every encoded block was round-trip checked before writing. This changes storage only, not the captured evidence. Small transcripts remain plain text. Raw tensor fixtures and source-bound executables remain in the bounded research directory; manifests bind their hashes. No model is installed or activated.

(r/'vq-2.1-quality-summary-v1.json').write_text(json.dumps(summary,indent=2)+'\n')

### run-vq-2.1-quality-v1.py

Original bytes: 2382. SHA-256: `6e493898f5765c51b656f217ff915662df73b591da34cd2f607a073089aa1492`.

Normalized bytes: 2382. SHA-256: `6e493898f5765c51b656f217ff915662df73b591da34cd2f607a073089aa1492`.

````text
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from types import SimpleNamespace
import hashlib,json,sys,time,subprocess
sys.path.insert(0,'Tools')
import quantization_logit_run as runner
r=Path('.build/quantization-research').resolve()
protocol=r/'vq-2.1-quality-protocol-v1.json'
p=json.loads(protocol.read_text());start=time.monotonic()
assert p['schema']==1 and p['candidate_revision']=='8684640a3956b01c47f5d47f9b999e2ab8b985f1'
assert json.loads((r/'vq-2.1-functional-run-v1.json').read_text())['complete']
bound=p['bound_files'];original=runner.supervise
receipt=r/'vq-2.1-quality-driver-v1.json'
assert not receipt.exists()
status={'schema':1,'started_at':datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),'protocol_sha256':runner.digest(protocol),'runs':[],'complete':False,'qualification':'unproven'}
def save(): receipt.write_text(json.dumps(status,indent=2)+'\n')
def check():
 assert all(runner.digest(Path(path))==sha for path,sha in bound.items()),'bound producer/input drift'
 assert int(subprocess.check_output(['du','-sk',str(r)],text=True).split()[0])*1024<350000000000
 assert sum(x.stat().st_size for x in r.rglob('*.f32'))<2000000000
 remaining=int(7200-(time.monotonic()-start));assert remaining>0
 return remaining
def bounded(command,directory,timeout):
 result=original(command,directory,min(1800,check()))
 check();status['runs'].append({'command':command,**result});save();return result
save();runner.supervise=bounded
runner.run(SimpleNamespace(arm='vq',inputs=r/'logit-pilot-v1-inputs/inputs.json',model=r/'candidate-2.1',out=r/'logit-pilot-v3-vq-2.1',inventory=r/'inventory-2.1/inventory.json',architecture=r/'qwen4_exp-pr1788.py',runtime=r/'inventory-3.2/model.py',order_proof=r/'vq-2.1-reference-order-v1/receipt.json',binary=None))
check()
command=[sys.executable,'Tools/quantization_logit_compare.py','--inputs',str(r/'logit-pilot-v1-inputs/inputs.json'),'--reference',str(r/'logit-pilot-v2-vq-4.4'),'--reference-inventory',str(r/'inventory-4.4/inventory.json'),'--baseline',str(r/'logit-pilot-v1-native'),'--candidate',str(r/'logit-pilot-v3-vq-2.1'),'--candidate-inventory',str(r/'inventory-2.1/inventory.json'),'--out',str(r/'logit-pilot-v3-comparison-2.1')]
status['comparison_command']=command;save();subprocess.run(command,check=True);check()
status['complete']=True;status['finished_at']=datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat();save()
````

      "text": "When a language model is larger than the available RAM, its weights can remain on SSD while a cache retains frequently used parts. A mixture-of-experts model routes each token to a subset of experts. The runtime reads missing experts, waits until their buffers are ready, and then computes the next layer. A smaller quantization can reduce both the bytes read and the space occupied by cached weights. This does not make speed proportional to file size: memory bandwidth, unpacking, GPU dispatch, cache misses, context length and competing applications also matter. The runtime must measure completed tokens over the full generation interval, including stalls and rejected speculative work. A saved memory ceiling remains the user's preference even when current system pressure requires a smaller allocation. After memory recovers, the runtime can grow again within that ceiling.",
      "text_sha256": "b9fa4a74b5191bc2c3d3ec91b63221a7f3a50f366ea174a9f64151019ae5edf8",
      "file": "memory-prose.json",

      "text": "# Merge overlapping half-open intervals. Empty intervals contribute no coverage.\ndef merge_intervals(intervals):\n    ordered = sorted((start, end) for start, end in intervals if start < end)\n    merged = []\n    for start, end in ordered:\n        if merged and start <= merged[-1][1]:\n            merged[-1] = (merged[-1][0], max(merged[-1][1], end))\n        else:\n            merged.append((start, end))\n    return merged\n\nassert merge_intervals([]) == []\nassert merge_intervals([(2, 2), (5, 8), (1, 4), (3, 6)]) == [(1, 8)]\n# Sorting dominates the running time; the scan processes each interval once.\n",
      "text_sha256": "6a28fbc2937ae105e170540b1bc49d388fe2e5f8d0b94c1047e5b8c85b82fd0a",
      "file": "python-interval.json",

      "text": "Una aplicación local debe distinguir entre la memoria física, el límite que guardó el usuario y la memoria que puede utilizar ahora.
